\le
\frac12\sum_{u\in\{v,v'\}}
\sum_{\emptyset\ne A\subset[n]}
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\prod_{i\in A}c_i\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\mathbb{E}_{x\in u+C}\prod_{i\in A}g_i(x_i)\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|.
\]
Fix a translate $u$. 
We bound the sum over $A$ in three cases, depending on
\[
t:=\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft\{i\in A:\rho_i>\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright\}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|.
\]


\begin{itemize}
\item Suppose $0<|A|\le m$. Forgetting phases and using $c_i\le1$, we proceed exactly as the proof of
\cref{lem:balanced-llr-below}: 
we apply \cref{lem:wftl} 
with $t=m$, $\gamma=2/\pi+O(p^{-2})$ via
\cref{cor:L-infty-bound}, and $\Gamma=1$ via Parseval.
These verifications hold uniformly over the biases. Since
$2k-m\sim\delta k\asymp n$,
\begin{equation}
\sum_{0<|A|\le m}
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\prod_{i\in A}c_i\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\mathbb{E}_{x\in u+C}\prod_{i\in A}g_i(x_i)\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|\le
\sum_{\substack{0\ne y\in C^\perp\\|y|\le m}}
\prod_{i\in\operatorname{supp}(y)}\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\widehat g_i(y_i)\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|
\le
n^{O(1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac2\pi\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)^k
=e^{-\Omega(n)}.
\end{equation}

\item Suppose $|A|>m$ and $t\le\varepsilon n$, let
\[
d:=\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left\lfloor\frac{3k-n}{2}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right\rfloor
\ge\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac14-\frac32\delta\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)n-O(1).
\]
For sufficiently small $\varepsilon,\delta$, there are at least
$d$ near-balanced coordinates in $A$, since
$|A|-t>m-\varepsilon n>d$. Choose $J$ of size $d$ among them.
For $j\in J$, we have $2\rho_j-1\in[0,\varepsilon]$.
Since $c_jg_j=2\mathbf{1}_{S_j}-2\rho_j$, \cref{lem:u3-gap}
and the triangle inequality give
\[
c_j\|g_j\|_{U^3}
\le
\|2\mathbf{1}_{S_j}-1\|_{U^3}+2\rho_j-1
\le q_0+\varepsilon.
\]
Moreover, $c_i\|g_i\|_\infty=2\rho_i$ is at most
$1+\varepsilon$ on near-balanced coordinates and at most two
everywhere.
Apply \cref{lem:block-u3} to $c_i g_i$ on $A$ and the constant function
one outside $A$, we obtain
\begin{align*}
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\prod_{i\in A}c_i\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\mathbb{E}_{x\in u+C}\prod_{i\in A}g_i(x_i)\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|\le
2^t(1+\varepsilon)^n(q_0+\varepsilon)^d\le
2^{\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left[
\varepsilon+\log_2(1+\varepsilon)
+\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac14-\frac32\delta\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
\log_2(q_0+\varepsilon)+o(1)
\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right]n}.
\end{align*}

\item Suppose $|A|>m$ and $t>\varepsilon n$, then
each of these $t$ coordinates satisfies
$c_i^2=4\rho_i(1-\rho_i)\le1-\varepsilon^2$.
Apply \cref{lem:bl-mds} to $u+C$, taking $h_i=|g_i|$ for
$i\in A$ and $h_i=\mathbf{1}$ otherwise.  We obtain
\[
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\prod_{i\in A}c_i\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\mathbb{E}_{x\in u+C}\prod_{i\in A}g_i(x_i)\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|
\le
\prod_{i\in A}
c_i\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\mathbb{E}|g_i|^{1/R}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)^R.
\]
Since $R\le1/2$ and $\mathbb{E}|g_i|^2=1$,
$\mathbb{E}|g_i|^{1/R}
\le
\|g_i\|_\infty^{1/R-2}\mathbb{E}|g_i|^2
=
\|g_i\|_\infty^{1/R-2}$.
Hence, as $c_i\|g_i\|_\infty\le2$,
\[
c_i\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\mathbb{E}|g_i|^{1/R}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)^R
\le
c_i\|g_i\|_\infty^{1-2R}
\le
2^{1-2R}c_i^{2R}.
\]
Multiplying over $A$ and recalling that $c_i^2\le1-\varepsilon^2$ for $t$-many coordinates $i$, we have
\[
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\prod_{i\in A}c_i\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\mathbb{E}_{x\in u+C}\prod_{i\in A}g_i(x_i)\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|
\le
2^{(1-2R)|A|}\prod_{i\in A}c_i^{2R}\le
2^{(1-2R)n}(1-\varepsilon^2)^{Rt}\le
2^{\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left[
2\delta+
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac12-\delta\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)\varepsilon
\log_2(1-\varepsilon^2)
\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right]n}.
\]
\end{itemize}

To sum the last two cases, \cref{eq:high-support-count}
bounds the number of supports with $|A|>m$ by
$2^{\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left[
H_2\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac52\delta-\delta^2\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)+o(1)
\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right]n}$.
Now, we choose $\delta,\varepsilon>0$ depending on constant $q_0 <1$. First choose $\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small that
\[
\varepsilon+\log_2(1+\varepsilon)
+\frac14\log_2(q_0+\varepsilon)<0.
\]
Then choose $\delta>0$ sufficiently small that
\begin{align*}
H_2\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac52\delta-\delta^2\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
+\varepsilon+\log_2(1+\varepsilon)
+\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac14-\frac32\delta\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
\log_2(q_0+\varepsilon)&<0,\\
H_2\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac52\delta-\delta^2\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
+2\delta+
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac12-\delta\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)\varepsilon
\log_2(1-\varepsilon^2)&<0.
\end{align*}
Both expressions are strictly negative at $\delta=0$.
Thus all three cases contribute $e^{-\Omega(n)}$, uniformly
over the leakage tuple and the translates, proving the claim.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- lemma-23: body_md -->
\label{lem:near-balanced-opi-below}
 There exists an absolute constant $\delta>0$ such that for every
generalized Reed-Solomon code $C\subset\mathbb{F}_p^n$ of fixed rate $R\ge1/2-\delta$ over a prime
field $\mathbb{F}_p$ with $p\ge n$, then uniformly over every OPI instance with common
density $\rho_i=\rho\ge1/2-\delta$ and $v\in\mathbb{F}_p^n$,
\[
\mathsf{F}_{\operatorname{OPI}}(v)\le e^{-\Omega(n)}.
\]
<!-- end lemma-23 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
For $R>1/2$, it follows \cref{lem:positive-opi-above}, so we fix
$R\in[1/2-\delta,1/2]$. Let $m:=\lfloor(2-\delta)k\rfloor$ and
$\lambda:=\sqrt{(1-\rho)/\rho}$. As in the proof of
\cref{lem:balanced-opi-below}, we split by $m$ the support size and bound
\[
\mathsf{F}_{\operatorname{OPI}}(v)
\le
\sum_{\substack{0\ne y\in C^\perp\\|y|\le m}}
\lambda^{|y|}
\prod_{i\in\operatorname{supp}(y)}|\widehat g_i(y_i)|
+
\sum_{|A|>m}
\lambda^{|A|}
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\mathbb{E}_{x\in v+C}\prod_{i\in A}g_i(x_i)\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|.
\]
We bound the two terms separately.
\begin{itemize}
\item For small supports, use the same local bounds as in the
proof of \cref{lem:positive-opi-above}:
\[
\sum_{\xi\ne0}\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\lambda|\widehat g_i(\xi)|\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)^2
=\frac{1-\rho}{\rho},
\qquad
\lambda|\widehat g_i(\xi)|
\le\frac{\sin(\pi\rho)}{\pi\rho}+O_\rho(p^{-2}).
\]
On the fixed interval $|\rho-1/2|\le\delta$, the first quantity
is bounded and the second has a uniform limiting bound below one.
Since $2k-m\sim\delta k\asymp n$, \cref{lem:wftl} gives
\[
\sum_{\substack{0\ne y\in C^\perp\\|y|\le m}}
\lambda^{|y|}
\prod_{i\in\operatorname{supp}(y)}|\widehat g_i(y_i)|
\le
n^{O(1)}
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac{\sin(\pi\rho)}{\pi\rho}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)^k
=e^{-\Omega(n)}.
\]

\item For large supports, fix $A$ with $|A|>m$.
The point is that the weighted functions themselves remain
close to balanced sign functions:
\[
\lambda g_i
=
\frac{\mathbf{1}_{S_i}-\rho}{\rho}
=
\frac{2\mathbf{1}_{S_i}-1-(2\rho-1)}{2\rho}
\implies
\lambda\|g_i\|_\infty
=
\max\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left\{1,\frac{1-\rho}{\rho}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right\}
\le
\frac{1+2\delta}{1-2\delta}.
\]
Fix $q_0<1$ from \cref{lem:u3-gap} with $\tau=1/4$.
Since $|2\rho-1|\le2\delta$, the same lemma and the triangle
inequality give
\[
\lambda\|g_i\|_{U^3}
\le
\frac{\|2\mathbf{1}_{S_i}-1\|_{U^3}+|2\rho-1|}{2\rho}
\le
\frac{q_0+2\delta}{1-2\delta}<1
\]
for sufficiently small $\delta$.
Let
\[
d:=\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left\lfloor\frac{3k-n}{2}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right\rfloor
\ge
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac14-\frac32\delta\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)n-O(1).
\]
Apply \cref{lem:block-u3}, using $\lambda g_i$ on $A$, the constant function
one outside $A$, and any $d$ coordinates from $A$ for the
$U^3$ bounds. We obtain
\[
\lambda^{|A|}
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\originalleft|\mathbb{E}_{x\in v+C}\prod_{i\in A}g_i(x_i)\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\originalright|
\le
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac{1+2\delta}{1-2\delta}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)^n
\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac{q_0+2\delta}{1-2\delta}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)^d.
\]
Counting supports by \cref{eq:high-support-count}, their total
contribution is at most
\[
2^{\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left[
H_2\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac52\delta-\delta^2\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
+\log_2\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac{1+2\delta}{1-2\delta}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
+\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac14-\frac32\delta\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
\log_2\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\mathopen{}\mathclose\bgroup\left(\frac{q_0+2\delta}{1-2\delta}\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right)
+o(1)
\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\aftergroup\egroup\right]n}.
\]
The limiting exponent at $\delta=0$ is
$\frac14\log_2q_0<0$. Choosing $\delta>0$ sufficiently small
therefore makes this contribution $e^{-\Omega(n)}$.
\end{itemize}
Combining the two estimates proves the lemma.
